Less than $1000 for a new gaming PC capable of 1080p gameplay

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

While Amazon's big sale has finished, Walmart and Best Buy are delivering the goods with huge deals on two different gaming PCs. Right now, you can pick up the ABS Cyclone M Aqua with an RTX 5060 for $999 at Walmart, or go for a CyberPowerPC rig with an RX 9060 for $999.99 at Best Buy instead.
● Grab the ABS machine at Walmart here
● Grab the CyberPowerPC machine at Best Buy here
These are budget-focused machines, and while some compromises do have to be made, the specs are pretty formidable for their price point. The ABS machine is fitted with an Intel Core i5-14400F, still a popular choice in lower-end machines like this one, along with 16GB of DDR4-3200 memory, a 1TB SSD, and most importantly, an Nvidia GeForce RTX 5060 8GB.
The RTX 5060 means you're getting high frame rates at 1080p, even with the highest graphics presets. DLSS support, meanwhile, gives you full access to Nvidia's bag of tricks, including multi-frame generation, which'll prove useful if you decide to go for 1440p or need an AI-generated push.
You also get an additional 2TB SSD with this deal, a Verbatim 2TB Vi7000 offering Gen 4 speeds. It's unclear whether or not this comes pre-installed, but installing an M.2 drive is an easy process, even for a beginner.
This ABS rig includes an Intel Core i5-14400F, Nvidia GeForce RTX 5060 8GB, 16GB DDR4-3200 RAM, and a 1TB SSD for storage. You also get an additional 2TB SSD included with this package.
On the flip side, you could go for the CyberPowerPC machine at Best Buy instead. It'll cost you 99c more, but instead you get a rig that includes the AMD Ryzen 7 8700F. This is an AM5 CPU, so you are better future-proofed here than the LGA 1700 socket used for the 14400F should you decide to upgrade the CPU later. It's also a newer, faster CPU, running with a 4.1 GHz base clock speed, boosting up to 5 GHz.
You also get the AMD Radeon RX 9060 here, also with 8GB VRAM. The RTX 5060 is faster, but this GPU isn't far off it in terms of raw power, and it'll still work well at the 1080p range you'll be focusing on with these specs. You're also getting faster DDR5 RAM here, also 16GB, with a 1TB SSD for Windows and any games.
Another solid rig for 1080p gaming, this machine includes the AMD Ryzen 7 8700F, alongside the AMD Radeon RX 9060, 16GB DDR5 RAM, and a 1TB SSD.
These are both good machines for 1080p gaming, with a price that puts them below $1,000. Given the current market, finding any rig priced this low with modern components is a struggle. The ABS Cyclone M Aqua for $999 is a good option with the RTX 5060, with DLSS support giving you an additional edge in-game, along with that extra 2TB SSD.
That said, the CyberPowerPC rig for $999.99 is a solid alternative, with newer components and better options for upgrades into the future. Either one will suit you fine for modern gaming, but these prices aren't likely to stick around for very long.

Microsoft is turning Windows 11 Search into a place for doing things, rather than just locating items. According to the official Windows Insider Blog, a new preview allows you to perform actions such as muting your PC, turning on Bluetooth, or arranging open windows directly from the taskbar's Search panel.
These new changes are rolling out to Windows Insiders in the Experimental channel, with new features to come in the future. Earlier, Microsoft's overhaul made Windows 11's Search more efficient by reducing visual clutter and giving users more control over web and Microsoft Store suggestions.
A tiny keyboard key gets a practical role in Windows 11, shortening deep folder journeys and speeding up troubleshooting.
As someone who uses Apple devices frequently, the resemblance to macOS Spotlight is hard to miss. Mac's search tool comes with the Actions view, which allows you to send messages, create notes, run shortcuts, and complete various other tasks without opening certain apps. Microsoft promises similar features, with the ability to use your phone to send messages said to be coming to Windows 11's Search soon.
For now, Microsoft is starting with simpler controls and gathering user feedback before releasing all the features. You can type phrases such as ""switch to dark mode"" or ""minimize all my windows,"" and Search will pull up an inline button for completing such requests. Moreover, Microsoft shows inline web previews for checking weather, stock prices, or other general inquiries — all done inside Search without the need to open a browser tab or tinker with a widget.
On my Mac, currency conversion is one of the most frequent inputs I throw into Spotlight. The screenshot below showcases how it works. Spotlight works almost instantly for general inquiries, and we can only hope Windows 11 manages to be as snappy and quick at delivering such results.
Thankfully, Microsoft says the redesigned Search is lighter and faster than before. Microsoft rebuilt it on WinUI 3 and claims its early testing shows “substantial improvements” in both performance and memory usage. The interface now places results in a single list instead of dominating much of the panel with a large preview pane — something that Microsoft itself admitted attracted few clicks.
More efficient memory usage seems to be in line with Microsoft's recent actions, as Windows 11 appears to be getting leaner with the latest 26H2 update. After installing the update, some users noticed up to 3GB less RAM usage at startup. Considering these recent changes to Windows 11, it seems like efficiency is finally receiving more attention.

69,2026-10-08,"Samsung is also one of the world's biggest smartphone makers
Samsung Electronics says it expects a nine-fold surge in its quarterly profits compared with a year earlier, driven by surging demand for memory chips used in artificial intelligence (AI) data centres.
The tech giant estimates that its operating profit for the three months to the end of September will jump to 107.4tn won (Â£61bn; $80bn), its fourth quarter in a row of record earnings.
Samsung is one of the world's largest memory chip makers alongside local rival SK Hynix and Micron in the US, which produce chips crucial for AI firms like Nvidia.
Samsung, which makes the Galaxy Fold and S26 smartphones, is also expected to get a boost from its latest folding devices that were launched in August.
The firm's full third-quarter earnings will be published at the end of October.
Some of South Korea's biggest companies release previews of their earnings to advise investors ahead of more detailed reports.
These forecasts are treated with much more credibility than estimates by external analysts as they are backed by internal company data.
There has been huge global demand for computer chips that power AI development, helping to lift the earnings and shares of manufacturers linked to the technology. 
The surge in demand in recent years has resulted in a shortage of semiconductors globally, pushing up sales for firms like Samsung, with its stock market valuation crossing $1tn (Â£757bn) earlier this year.
Investment in the industry has surged, with US tech giants including Google, Amazon and Meta pledging to pour more than $650bn into AI projects this year.
In June, South Korea unveiled plans for at least $880bn in projects led by Samsung and SK Hynix to build out the country's chip manufacturing capabilities in the coming years.
Rival Asian firms in Japan, China and Taiwan are also investing heavily in chip plants as demand for AI soars.
A chip shortage driven by surging demand has led firms including Samsung to raise prices, making products such as smartphones and computers more expensive.

71,2026-10-08,"Asos has told its customers that hackers are in possession of detailed profiles of potentially millions of the online store's users.
It issued the update after BBC News told the retailer it had been contacted by cyber criminals who said this week's breach went beyond the ""basic contact details"" Asos previously said might have been accessed.
Names, addresses, phone numbers, emails, customer numbers and dates of birth are now in the hands of criminals.
One victim told the BBC it was ""very unsettling"" that the hackers now know these things about her.
The criminals also have access to the searches customers have made on the website. Terms like ""reclaimed vintage"", ""glamorous wide fit"" and ""Asos petite"" are visible in the data.
Harriet has been using Asos since 2019 - another detail the hackers now know. 
""What I find particularly worrying is the possibility that stolen data can be used as a tool for for future attacks, meaning the impact of a breach could extend well beyond the initial incident,"" she said.
With the stolen information, scammers may be able to create convincing phishing attack emails or phone calls.
The risk to individuals is now higher and customers are being warned about potential impersonation scams.
In its email to customers, Asos confirmed data profiles were taken but said no bank details or passwords were accessed.
""Please remain cautious of unexpected messages or calls claiming to be from Asos,"" it said. 
""We will never ask you to share passwords, security codes or payment details through an unsolicited message or call.""
The company did not respond to questions about the scale of the breach.
Customers saw this pop up notification on Tuesday
The high profile hack made global headlines on Tuesday when cyber criminals used Asos's own app system to send a pop up notification to potentially millions of people.
Later that day the firm confirmed to shareholders via the London Stock Exchange that the pop up was sent by an ""unauthorised third party"" and ""basic personal information including name and contact details may have been accessed.""
The company then sent an email to customers with similar wording.
On Wednesday evening the cyber criminals responsible contacted the BBC sharing a sample of the stolen data which showed the true extent of the hack.
The BBC held off publishing this article to allow Asos to contact its customers first.
Asos said it is still investigating the data breach and it would ""contact customers directly where we believe additional information, support or action may be required"".
The UK fashion site explained to customers that hackers gained access to an Asos employee account by ""impersonating a trusted contact to obtain log in credentials"".
With that log in to an unnamed service, the hackers were able to download the customer data.
In the pop up notification send to customers by the hackers, they claimed they had ""compromised the Snowflake instance"".
Snowflake is a popular data storage and analysis company whose customers have been breached in the past due to unauthorised log ins.
The cyber criminals, calling themselves Xuanyewen, claimed to the BBC they used a platform which is built natively on top of Snowflake - called Simon AI - to gain access to the data.
Simon AI has been contacted for comment. Snowflake previously said its platform had not been breached. 
Asos said customers are not being asked to take any action. 
But cyber security experts have warned users to change passwords as a precaution and be on alert for suspicious activity.
""Passwords have not been stolen, so be highly suspicious of any unsolicited text or email asking you to change or share yours,"" said Trevor Dearing, Senior Director of Critical Infrastructure at Illumio.
""Expect scammers to mention the attack, use your personal details to seem genuine, and create urgency, such as threatening to lock your account within 24 hours.""
Asos said its website and app are safe to use and ""we know our customers trust us with their information"". 
""We take that responsibility seriously and have already taken additional steps to further strengthen security controls,"" it said.

72,2026-10-08,"
  By Anthony Franco |
  at October 7, 2026 10:43pm CDT  
With the regular season in the books, MLBTR is taking a position-by-position look at the 2026-27 free agent market. All that remains: a look at the bullpen market. We’ll begin with the lefties, a smaller group that nonetheless includes the best reliever who’ll be available.
Player ages are for the 2027 season, and names within each tier are listed alphabetically, not “ranked.” Only players who appeared in MLB (or on major league 60-day injured lists) this year are included.
Previous entries in this series: Catcher, First Base, Second Base, Third Base, Shortstop, Center Field, Corner Outfield, Designated Hitter, Starting Pitcher
Top of the Class
Morejon is the best non-closing reliever to hit free agency in years. He’s an elite setup man who presumably would’ve been a high-end closer if he hadn’t shared a bullpen with Robert Suarez and Mason Miller.
The Cuban-born Morejon will hit the market coming off a third straight sub-3.00 ERA season. He worked a career-high 86 innings (third-most of any reliever in MLB) with a 2.83 earned run average. Morejon punched out more than 30% of opponents while running an elite 58% ground-ball rate. He kept his walks to a manageable 8% clip. Morejon’s fastball averaged 99 mph, making him the hardest-throwing southpaw in the game. Oh, he’s also a former top prospect who made his MLB debut at 20 and accordingly is hitting the market before his 28th birthday.
To the extent there’s any concern with Morejon, it’s probably the injury history. He moved to the bullpen largely because he couldn’t stay healthy over his first couple seasons. Morejon underwent Tommy John surgery in 2021 and continued to battle elbow discomfort through the ’23 season. He’s avoided the injured list for the last three years, seemingly putting the health concerns behind him. His status is a little up in the air again after he was unavailable for last night’s Game 3 of the NLDS due to a balky shoulder. That said, the Padres haven’t taken him off the postseason roster and there’s no indication he’s facing anything serious.
The Padres could issue Morejon a $23.15MM qualifying offer. A single-inning reliever has never accepted the QO. It’s typically reserved for closers (e.g. Josh Hader, Edwin Díaz, Raisel Iglesias), but Morejon is so talented that the Padres should be comfortable making the QO — presuming he’s healthy at season’s end. Though he’ll have a hard time matching Díaz’s record-setting $23MM average annual value over multiple seasons, Morejon is younger and arguably better than Tanner Scott was when the latter commanded four years and $72MM from the Dodgers.
Solid Setup Arms
One could reasonably argue for having Bubic in the swingman section or excluding him from a reliever preview altogether. He’s been a starter for the bulk of his career, only moving to short relief after the Dodgers acquired him from Kansas City at the trade deadline. Bubic may well seek out another rotation opportunity in free agency.
Durability has always been the question more than talent. Bubic underwent Tommy John surgery in 2023 and was limited to relief work in the second half of ’24. He excelled over four months in a return to the rotation in 2025 until suffering a season-ending rotator cuff strain. This year, elbow and shoulder soreness knocked him out from the middle of May until the final couple weeks of the regular season. Bubic fired five scoreless appearances in short relief to earn a spot on L.A.’s playoff roster.
Minter rebounded from 2025 lat surgery with a solid season between the Mets and Twins. He combined for a 2.56 ERA across 45 2/3 frames after beginning the year on the shelf. Minter’s velocity picked up later in the year with Minnesota, and while he struggled with home runs, he had some of the best control of his career. He’s unlikely to match the two-year, $22MM contract he commanded during his last trip through free agency but won’t have any problem finding a big league deal.
Okert has found a second act since signing a minor league deal with the Astros over the 2024-25 offseason. He’s coming off a 2.13 ERA with a strong 26% strikeout percentage and modest 5.4% walk rate through 67 2/3 innings. Okert doesn’t throw hard but has befuddled hitters with a low-80s breaking ball he throws two-thirds of the time. He has a chance at two years entering his age-35 season.
Puk fits in this group on talent. Between 2022-25, the former sixth overall pick combined for a 3.38 ERA while striking out 30% of batters faced. Puk has barely pitched over the past two seasons, though, undergoing UCL surgery last April and suffering a capsule sprain in his throwing shoulder this August. It’s probably going to be an incentive-laden, one-year deal.
Raley works mostly with a low-80s breaking ball and a mid-80s cutter to generate weak contact. He turned in excellent results for the Mets from 2025-26 before scuffling after a deadline trade to Philadelphia. Raley finished the season with a 3.25 ERA and average strikeout and walk marks in 52 2/3 innings. He’ll be on one-year deals for the rest of his career.
Romero has a decent chance at a two-year contract after his fourth straight solid campaign. He pitched into leverage spots with the Cardinals and has managed a 3.36 ERA or lower in three straight seasons. This year, Romero combined for 56 1/3 frames between the Cards and Brewers with a 3.36 mark, an average strikeout rate, and a lot of ground-balls.
Soto has plus velocity from the left side, averaging 97 mph on his four-seam and 96 on his sinker. He signed a one-year, $7.75MM free agent deal with the Pirates last offseason. Soto had a decent season, recording a 3.63 ERA while striking out nearly 28% of opponents and collecting 16 saves. Soto’s entire career has been a bit of a roller coaster as his command comes and goes. The inconsistency might limit him to another one-year term.
Vesia has a good shot at the second-largest contract among lefty relievers. An unheralded trade pickup by the Dodgers in 2021, he’s been a consistent presence at the back of Dave Roberts’ bullpens six years running. Vesia churned out another strong season, leading L.A. with 20 holds while turning in a 2.64 ERA through 58 innings.
It’s a bit of an unconventional profile. Vesia doesn’t have overpowering velocity, averaging 92 mph on his fastball. Yet no pitcher generates the kind of induced vertical break (essentially ride at the top of the strike zone) that Vesia does. Batters struggle to square it up, year in and year out. Vesia has fanned upwards of 30% of opponents in three straight seasons and gets a ton of weak fly balls from hitters who expect the heater to be an inch or two lower than it arrives at the plate.
Despite the fringy velocity, it’s a stuff over command profile. Vesia can get himself into trouble with free passes and walked more than 13% of opponents this year. He was dialed into the zone during a dominant September and has started the postseason well. Vesia should have plenty of interest on multi-year contracts and might pull a three-year deal at an eight figure salary.
Specialists/Middle Relief
Alvardo probably has the highest ceiling of anyone in this group. He’s been a key leverage reliever in the past but was erratic virtually all season for Philadelphia. Alvarado stumbled to a 5.86 ERA over 58 1/3 innings. His 25.6% strikeout rate was a little above average but down a few points from his career marks. Alvarado still sits close to 98 mph with his sinker but had career-low whiff and ground-ball rates. The stuff is going to net him a big league contract, yet he’s looking at a one-year deal.
Burke surely secured himself another big league contract with a solid year in Cincinnati. He worked 74 1/3 frames of 2.30 ERA ball while only blowing one lead all season. Burke sits around 96 mph and gets a lot of weak contact. His strikeout and walk profile was middling, so he’s probably looking at a one-year contract.
Hill is a submariner ground-ball specialist. He had a low-3.00s ERA in 2024 and ’25 before a home run spike jumped him to a 4.60 mark over 58 2/3 innings. No pitcher in MLB gets more ground-balls, though, which should be enough for Hill to find a cheap big league deal.
Mayza held a bullpen spot for the Phillies all season after signing a minor league contract. He had a nice year, pitching to a 3.48 ERA with a 24% strikeout rate over 67 1/3 innings. Mayza sits around 94 mph and held lefty batters to a .231/.272/.343 line across 115 plate appearances. He probably did enough to earn a guaranteed contract this time around.
It’s a similar story to Hill with Milner, a soft-tossing sidearmer who has made a career off weak contact. Milner’s ground-ball rate was just alright this year, though he still managed a 3.64 ERA over 42 innings. The Cubs released him in late August and he finished the season in Seattle.
Rogers is a former All-Star closer who returned to the Twins as an elder statesman this year. He tossed 60 1/3 innings of 4.33 ERA ball with mediocre strikeout and walk numbers. He’s a borderline candidate for another big league deal.
Thilebar had an excellent season for the Cubs in 2025. A home run spike this year left him with a 4.86 ERA across 50 innings. The soon to be 40-year-old Thielbar didn’t lose any velocity and still punched out 28.3% of opposing hitters.
Long Relievers/Swing Options
The Rangers signed Alexander for $1.125MM last offseason. It was a nice pickup, as he tossed 73 innings of 3.70 ERA ball. He doesn’t miss many bats but got ground-balls at a near-50% clip. Alexander’s numbers faded down the stretch, yet the TCU product could return to Arlington in a similar role for barely more than the league minimum.
Newcomb has made good on a $4.5MM free agent deal with the White Sox. He worked to a 3.09 ERA with league average strikeout and walk rates while absorbing 90 1/3 frames over 60 appearances. His fastball is back up into the 95 mph range after sitting around 93 for the A’s in 2025. Newcomb should comfortably find another big league deal and has a chance at a two-year contract, especially if he finds a club willing to let him compete for their fifth starter job.
Suter had his typical season, churning out results behind a ton of soft contact. The soft-tossing Harvard product pitched to a 3.36 ERA despite a modest 20% strikeout rate and sub-90 mph fastball. Suter combined for 91 innings between the Angels and Braves and should secure another modest MLB deal.
Yarbrough worked almost exclusively mop-up spots with the Yankees. He turned in 63 1/3 frames of 3.69 ERA ball with an 18% strikeout rate. His fastball is down to 87 mph on average. He’s an innings eating long reliever.
Player Options
This could be an interesting call for Matsui, who has progressively improved over three years in the majors. He turned in a sterling 1.83 ERA across 64 frames this year to emerge as the Padres’ second-best lefty behind Morejon. Matsui, 31 this month, doesn’t have big velocity (92 mph fastball) but can rack up whiffs with an excellent splitter and slider. The problem is that he’s too infrequently in the zone, walking almost 14% of opponents. It seems likelier he’ll opt in, but Matsui would probably find a similar contract to the two years and $13.5MM remaining on his deal if he tested the market.
Peralta has bypassed opt-out opportunities on his four-year deal with San Diego in the last two offseasons. He’s likely to do the same here, as there’s limited contractual upside beyond his $4.45MM salary. Peralta ate 77 1/3 innings of 3.61 ERA ball but only struck out 15% of opponents and has been left off San Diego’s playoff rosters.
Club Options
Hart has earned $1MM and $1.2MM in each of the past two seasons with San Diego while compiling a 5.30 ERA in a swing role. The Friars have shuttled him between Triple-A and the majors, but he burned his final minor league option year in 2026. San Diego may want him back, but probably on a minor league deal as opposed to a guaranteed $2.5MM. The buyout seems likely.
Minor League Deal/Reclamation Types

73,2026-10-08,"The storm is forecast to strike Florida, Alabama and Mississippi with up to 10 inches of rain this weekend.
Save
Share
Hurricane Isaias has intensified into the first hurricane of the Atlantic season as it barrels over record-warm water toward the northern shoreline of the United States Gulf Coast, prompting school closures and local government shutdowns.
According to the Miami-based National Hurricane Center, the storm is forecast to make landfall in parts of the Florida Panhandle, southern Alabama and southern Mississippi late Friday or early Saturday. The low-lying areas are especially vulnerable to flooding and storm surges.
The center warned residents in likely impact zones on Thursday to complete all emergency life-safety and property protections without delay.
It has also issued a hurricane watch from Bay St. Louis, Mississippi, through Indian Pass in the northwestern part of Florida in anticipation of torrential downpours and the substantial risk of localised flash and urban flooding.
A rainfall total of 3-6 inches is forecast with isolated peaks up to 10 inches.
 

 
“We are expecting Isaias to be a dangerous hurricane when it makes landfall,” Michael Brennan, director of the National Hurricane Center, said in a Thursday morning update, adding that he expects “up to near-major hurricane strength” by Friday as the storm pounds north of Mexico’s Yucatan Peninsula.
On Thursday morning, Isaias was packing maximum sustained winds of nearly 130km/h (80mph) with higher gusts. Heavy rain and flash flooding also threaten the inland areas throughout the weekend, extending as far as the Tennessee Valley and potentially encompassing a broad stretch from the inland southeast up through the southern Appalachian Mountains.
Ahead of the storm, oil and gas producers shut down a significant share of offshore production in the US Gulf, which accounts for almost 15 percent of crude pumped annually in the US.
As of Wednesday, about 25 percent of the oil production in the region had been temporarily halted, taking about 511,000 barrels a day off the market, according to the Marine Minerals Administration. It said eight of the Gulf’s 371 manned oil platforms had been evacuated.
With oil prices already high, the production cuts are steep enough to affect markets, though other Gulf hurricanes have caused larger disruptions, said Jim Burkhard, global head of crude oil market research for S&P Global Energy. More concerning, he said, is whether Isaias strikes as a powerful storm near refineries.
When Isaias was first declared a hurricane on Wednesday, it marked the latest date for an Atlantic season’s first hurricane in 121 years, falling just short of the record set in 1905, according to data from the National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration. The season normally runs from June 1 through November 30.

75,2026-10-08,"Securitize’s new offering gives eligible investors access to tokenized shares of 12 US companies, with dividend and voting rights and plans for round-the-clock trading.
Securitize shares climbed about 10% Thursday as the company launched tokenized versions of major US stocks on Solana, offering eligible investors exposure to equities backed by underlying shares with dividend and voting rights.
The new offering, called Securitize Stocks, includes tokenized shares of Apple, Nvidia, Microsoft, Tesla and eight other US-listed companies. The tokens are backed one-for-one by underlying shares and will initially trade during extended market hours through Securitize’s registered broker-dealer platform, according to Thursday’s announcement.
Securitize (SECZ) stock. Source: Yahoo Finance
Securitize said the tokens represent security entitlements under Article 8 of the Uniform Commercial Code and preserve applicable shareholder benefits, including dividends and voting rights. The company also plans to make the assets available on tokenized securities platforms being developed by the New York Stock Exchange and OKXICE, though neither has set a launch date.
The tokens will settle in USDC on Solana, with Jump Trading providing liquidity through market-making services. 
Trading will begin during extended market hours, with plans to expand to 24/7 trading on an unspecified timeline. 
Beyond trading, the company is exploring broader uses for the tokenized equities. According to Securitize, Ripple Prime plans to support the launch and explore incorporating the assets into its institutional trading services, while decentralized lending platform Aave has been identified as a potential venue for using the stocks as collateral.
Ripple Prime did not respond to Cointelegraph’s request for additional details by publication time.
Related: Securitize Capital becomes SEC-registered investment adviser
Thursday marked the second sharp intraday rally for Securitize shares this week. The stock climbed nearly 8% in early trading Tuesday, when the company announced a partnership with South Korean technology firm LG CNS to explore tokenized funds, equities and stablecoins for financial institutions.
The South Korean agreement also covers infrastructure that could connect local financial institutions with global capital markets. It follows a September memorandum of understanding with Dubai’s Virtual Assets Regulatory Authority to support regulated tokenization initiatives in Dubai.
Securitize shares have gained roughly 54% over the past month, according to Yahoo Finance data.
The company’s expansion comes amid broader growth in tokenized real-world assets. RWA.xyz data shows distributed asset value excluding stablecoins reached $38.88 billion as of Thursday, up from $25.36 billion a year earlier.

Microsoft’s new Surface Laptop Ultra looks ready for serious AI work

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Microsoft has unveiled its most powerful laptop device yet - the Surface Laptop Ultra, with shipments scheduled to begin on October 16 2026.
The device will cost from $2,599, putting it directly into the price range of high-end machines used by creators and technical professionals.
Microsoft is also offering configurations costing $3,699.99 and $5,899.99, giving the Surface Laptop Ultra specifications aimed at demanding professional workloads
Microsoft says the laptop is designed for creators, AI specialists, engineers, scientists, developers, and other users running demanding applications.
The entry configuration comes with 24GB RAM and 512GB storage, while the next version has 32GB RAM and 1TB storage.
The highest configuration includes 128GB RAM and 1TB storage, while every model uses the same 15-inch mini-LED display.
Its hardware combines Nvidia’s RTX Spark platform with an N1X processor and RTX Blackwell graphics, supporting up to 128GB unified memory.
Sign up to the TechRadar Pro newsletter to get all the top news, opinion, features and guidance your business needs to succeed!
The laptop can run demanding games, but Microsoft is presenting it primarily around professional applications rather than gaming performance.
Adobe is adapting Premiere Pro and Photoshop for RTX Spark, while a demonstration showed an optimized Premiere Pro version rendering a scene faster.
That software support matters because applications must be adapted properly before users can fully benefit from the platform’s Arm-based architecture.
Unfortunately, the laptop lacks ConnectX-7 and QSFP ports, limiting direct high-speed connectivity with DGX Spark systems that use Nvidia networking hardware.
That omission could be deliberate, since adding dedicated high-speed networking would raise hardware costs and make the laptop more closely aligned with Nvidia’s specialized systems.
This could matter for AI professionals who expect the Surface Laptop Ultra to connect directly with other Nvidia systems for high-speed development workloads.
Microsoft says the laptop is designed for creators, AI specialists, engineers, scientists, developers and other users running demanding applications.
The Surface Laptop Ultra has a large trackpad, premium display and professional design that resemble features found on Apple’s MacBook Pro.
Its specifications also place it against high-end professional laptops rather than mainstream Windows machines designed mainly for everyday productivity.
Microsoft’s intended users include professionals who need substantial memory, graphics capability and software support for AI, development and content creation.
Adobe’s work on Premiere Pro and Photoshop could become important because application support will influence how useful RTX Spark becomes for professional users.
The laptop’s high memory ceiling also gives the highest configuration capacity suited to workloads that require substantial local resources.
However, its connectivity choices create a distinction between the Surface Laptop Ultra and Nvidia’s dedicated development hardware.
That limitation is particularly relevant when considering the laptop as an AI development machine rather than simply another high-end professional computer.
Microsoft is therefore entering Apple’s professional laptop space with powerful hardware, but its Nvidia platform also comes with connectivity limitations.

College Football Playoff leaders are channeling their best Jerry Maguire this week.
""Show me the money!"" they might tell you – or at least that's what they're asking their current television network partner.
Friday marks the end of a 30-day exclusive negotiating window between ESPN and the CFP around the rights to an expanded 24-team field. That won’t, however, be the end of all this.
While Friday could bring an offer from ESPN — also not guaranteed — if no deal is agreed to, the CFP is obligated to bring its own offer to ESPN. The network would then have five business days for negotiations to continue, likely leading to an actual deadline of Oct. 19 (Monday is a federal holiday).
The shorter version is this: CFP leaders are deciding whether to give ESPN the new inventory created by an expanded playoff, or take it to the open market and seek the highest bidder.
How does this all work? It’s complicated and nuanced. Here's what I'm hearing and where things stand:
Conversations about an expanded playoff have circulated for the better part of two years.
Initially the brainchild of Big Ten Commissioner Tony Petitti, he saw it as a way to add value to the regular season while also providing more postseason opportunities for his conference’s programs. Petitti’s initial pitch also included automatic qualifiers and other measures of guaranteed spots.
That won’t be the case here.
Big Ten Commissioner Tony Petitti is leading the push for a 24-team CFP. (Photo by Michael Reaves/Getty Images) 
The current CFP contract guarantees ESPN maintains the rights for up to 14 teams without having to add any new money to the existing deal. 
A 16-team format was also bandied about, but such a shift would almost certainly necessitate the elimination of conference championship games — contests that bring in between $200-250 million between the Power Four leagues — and adding just two new CFP games certainly wouldn’t bring that kind of value to justify the move.
Sources briefed on the matter told me the focus remains on a 24-team format that would be essentially all at-large bids and one guaranteed spot for the Group of Six.
The addition of 12 new teams would require an expansion of the first round and the creation of a second round, both of which would likely be played on campus, while the traditional New Year’s Six bowl sites would also be integrated into the quarterfinal and semifinal slots. 
In all, expanding to 24 teams would bring a total of 10 new games to the market (eight first round, two second round).
American Conference Commissioner Tim Pernetti had been among those spearheading a push in recent months for an additional guaranteed spot for the Group of Six should the field size double, but sources involved in negotiations told me that almost certainly won’t happen.
As I reported earlier this week, an expanded CFP would also likely see the Army-Navy game moved to an exclusive window on what is now conference championship Saturday the first week of December, while first-round games could begin the second week of December.
Conversations between CFP stakeholders and ESPN have basically centered on how much money adding eight new games to the field might fetch.
That said, there are layers of nuance to the discussion.
ESPN currently controls the bulk of the FBS conference championship games. That gives the network about $200-250 million that it’s already invested in the ecosystem. How much might be added on top of that? That’s less clear, and those involved have been tight-lipped.
For discussion’s sake, let’s do a bit of hypothetical, back of the napkin math.
Let’s say the eight CFP games — which are essentially extra first and second round games between teams ranked No. 13-24 — would fetch $500 million, a little less than half the value of the current deal. Between $200-250 million of that is already spoken for in making leagues whole on the conference title games.
The distribution of the extra $250-ish million becomes its own fight. The SEC and Big Ten shifted the finances of CFP distribution under the terms of the new CFP television deal such that they each receive around 29% of annual revenue, while the ACC (17.25%), Big 12 (14.75%) and Group of Six/Independents (9%) receive smaller cuts.
SEC commissioner Greg Sankey and Big Ten Commissioner Tony Petitti have the bulk of control over the future format of the CFP. (Photo by Jamie Schwaberow/NCAA Photos via Getty Images) 
Sources suggest the Big 12 and ACC would, naturally, desire a more equal split of any new money. Whether the Big Ten and SEC will actually go for that is a separate conversation for a later date and those involved aren't splitting hairs over cash that hasn't been promised yet.
There’s one other caveat in all of this. ESPN operates the CFP’s sponsorship program. If the games were to go out to another bidder, how might that work? That’s not altogether certain.
Sources briefed on the conversations told me ESPN had yet to bring forward an offer as of the middle of this week, while a call is currently scheduled on Friday among CFP leaders to discuss any potential deal. The CFP also technically has until Dec. 1 to make any changes to the field in time for the 2028 rendition, though that deadline could be moved if extra time is needed.
A decision may not actually be made on Friday given the subsequent five-day window, but we might finally have an answer that has eluded decision makers for months: How much are eight new CFP games worth?

Dabo Swinney And Clemson Are Stuck In Neutral After Another Blowout Loss"
78,2026-10-08,"God tier haul
Viewed from the front, my truck, with its winch and thick off-road tires, could be straight from SnowRunner central casting. Sure, it would an off-off-Broadway production of SnowRunner, with the polycount and texture resolution of a PS1-era take on the driving simulator, but I'm looking at a vehicle that is ready to struggle through thick mud and deep water; a petrol-guzzling workhorse.
Then I walk around the side and glimpse the load it's hauling: a giant severed hand, freshly cut from the corpse of a buried god.
Prospice: Anomalous Logistics has all the markings of SnowRunner, MudRunner and Spintires games, the driving simulators with a focus on overcoming difficult off-road terrain, but it's seen through the lens of eldritch horror and the new weird.
 


Prospice: Announcement Trailer | Open Playtest Available Now



Watch on YouTube

Unlike the horror movies that keep their monsters out of shot for the first half, letting audiences stew on the possible, Prospice has no qualms about putting them front and centre. The demo opens with you facing a chap in a white hazmat suit, the black eyes and mask looking almost like a bemused smile, while over his shoulder you can see the rotting corpse of a humanoid creature. If the thing were standing it would easily tower over you, three or four storeys tall.
You mustn't look too closely, mind. Stare at the remains too long and a bar appears at the bottom of your HUD. Let it fill and your 'Life pattern fractures', or, put another way, you get deaded by means of insanity. Besides, you shouldn't be hanging about the eldritch corpse anyway, you've a job to do.
You're one of the Fenrir Directive's Pathfinders. The shady government-funded group that has operational control over the Nastrondey Island, charged with investigating the occult-y goings ons, and the Pathfinders are the delivery drivers that ferry all the staff, equipment, and ""findings"" between bases. Apparently, Fenrir Directive tried doing this work with remote vehicles and ran into quite a few technical problems, so they now rely on good old fashioned humans.
Which brings me back to the truck and its load. I'm to drive it to a research station for drop off.
Behind the wheel of the truck, Prospice really does feel a lot like SnowRunner. You've got to disengage the parking brake, move between high and low gears, and manually shift into reverse. You can also switch into All-Wheel-Drive when you hit particularly steep or muddy terrain. Though, as in Saber's driving games, through everything you need to keep an eye on your fuel gauge. Though, at least from what I've seen in the demo, the dangers of running out of fuel are significantly higher in Prospice.
As someone who has sunk a lot of hours into the mud of Spintires, SnowRunner, and MudRunner, driving over the deforming terrain in Prospice captures that same laborious-yet-satisfying physicality. The soil gives way under your vehicle, leaving deep treadmarks in your wake. Any time you need to cross deep water there is a risk of getting stuck in the silt. Still, if you do become wedged somewhere, you can hop out of the truck, unwind your winch, and look for a good spot to plant its anchor, before pulling yourself out of the quagmire. I don't think Prospice's fantastical elements would work so well without that attention to the muscular reality of the driving.
Where Prospice leaves its source behind and drives into new territory is by introducing uncertainty into your tools. Pressing tab will bring up a map with a marker showing your position, but it's often inaccurate. The signal you send and receive to the nearby triangulation towers warping on its journey. At one point I was driving along a riverside track, hauling a crate to a distant hill. On the map, my locator was showing me far across the river in a distant field. A tutorial pop up makes clear you will need to rely on landmarks to keep your bearings.
The cargo I carried also helps with the mapping issue. It was something called a Navspire, a staff I can plant in the ground that appears as a marker on the map. Unlike my signal, that can bounce around, these are reliable humanmade landmarks to track your position. At first, I thought the device was just a stick with a warm green light on top, but as I look closer it also seems to be strung with bones that catch the wind as it blows. Best not to question the mechanics of these devices too much, they do the job.
Another departure from Saber's games is that you can get out of the vehicle and walk around the world. Though, the further you get from your truck the weaker your signal gets and the fuzzier your screen becomes until it's virtually impossible to see what's in front of you. On one excursion where I just couldn't mount the hill, even with the help of low gear, all-wheel drive, and the winch, I got out and walked. Up the hilltop, I could make out a glowing red spot in the distance, but as I moved nearer to the object and further from the truck, my vision deteriorated. When I was close enough to pick it up I could barely make it out, but as I walked back my vision cleared and I saw I was holding a red marble: a teardrop of a god.
A demo is only a glimpse of a game, but this combination of off-road driving and weird fiction immediately worked for me. Even though I had one frustrating moment where I got irretrievably stuck on top of a rock and had to restart from my last save, I still came away hooked on seeing more of this world and what horrors I'll uncover in the full game.

Prospice: Anomalous Logistics


        PC, PS5, Xbox Series X/S

Nature Communications
                             (2026) Cite this article


                            We’re sharing this article early to provide faster access to peer-reviewed, accepted research. It is citable and carries a permanent DOI. This version is subject to further edits and will be replaced automatically by the final Version of Record. All legal disclaimers apply.
                        
Response rates to immune checkpoint blockade (ICB) in ovarian cancer are low. In preclinical studies, poly(ADP-ribose) polymerase inhibition (PARPi) synergized with CTLA4 ICB to achieve long-term survival in Brca1-deficient ovarian cancer models. Here we evaluated the safety and efficacy of combined treatment with the PARPi olaparib and the CTLA4 ICB tremelimumab for recurrent epithelial ovarian cancer in a multi-site single-arm phase I/II study enrolling 50 women with germline BRCA1 or BRCA2 mutations (NCT02571725). In phase I, no new toxicities were identified (n = 3). In the combined phase I and II safety cohort (n = 49), 57% of subjects experienced grade 3 or 4 non-hematologic toxicity, with tremelimumab discontinuation in 34.7%. The primary phase II endpoint, overall response rate was 32.7% (95% CI 19.5-45.8%), and disease control rate was 47.7% (95% CI 33.0-62.5%). Secondary outcomes were progression-free survival (PFS, 3.0 months, 95% CI 2.7-5.9), and overall survival (24.3 months, CI 14.9-30.4). In exploratory post-hoc biomarker analyses, low VSTM5 or IFIT1B expression was associated with treatment benefit and improved PFS (p < 0.001). Although the trial did not meet its prespecified efficacy endpoint, PFS > 12 months in 6 subjects among this heavily pre-treated cohort highlight the need for biomarkers to select regimens for patients with recurrent ovarian cancer.
We wish to thank the University of New Mexico clinical trials team including Leslie Byatt, Christine Gan, PhD, Abigail Ford, Ellen Wojcek, Gabriella Quintana, Mollie Dedig, Kaylee Deutsch Armijo, Sheri Westgate, and Valerie Parks, as well as Cathleen Martinez from the UNM Human Tissue Repository. We also wish to thank Dr. Sandra Orsulic at the University of California, Los Angeles, and Dr. Iain McNeish at the Imperial College of London for kindly providing the cell lines described. Finally, we thank Oluoma M. Edeh from the University of New Mexico for assistance with data deposition.
The clinical trial was funded and approved by AstraZeneca, however all data collection, analysis, reporting, and manuscript writing were performed independently by the study team. SFA discloses support for the research from the NCI [R37CA229221, P30CA118100], the V Foundation, and the University of New Mexico Comprehensive Cancer Center [Shared Resources Pilot Grant, Early Phase Clinical Research Grant].
Deceased: Dennis McCance.
Division of Gynecologic Oncology, University of New Mexico Comprehensive Cancer Center, Albuquerque, NM, USA

The league body has increased distributions by Â£7.6m, an 18% rise, with the bulk of money coming from broadcasting deals, licensing, match data and sponsorship.
""In an increasingly competitive international rights market, these results are testament to the enduring attraction of the SPFL,"" said chief executive Neil Doncaster.
""These are by far the strongest financial results in the SPFL's history, with turnover and total group distributions both surpassing the records set last year, and we are determined to maintain that momentum.""
Scottish Premiership crowds topped four million for the first time last season as the league continues to attract the highest per capita attendances in Europe.
Figures published by Uefa showed approximately six million Scottish supporters watched men's matches during the 2025-26 campaign, with 900,000 in the lower leagues, 200,000 at domestic cup ties and 800,000 at European matches.
""Successfully balancing broadcast revenue with match-day attendances and the unique atmosphere created within packed stadia is central to the SPFL being recognised widely as one of the most intense, compelling, and emotive sporting competitions in the world,"" added Doncaster.

84,2026-10-08,"Visa probe also targets universities for allegedly exploiting international students to reduce US wage costs.
Save
Share
The United States government is suspending several major tech companies, including Adobe and Microsoft, from a programme that gives skilled foreign workers a path to permanent residency amid allegations of fraud.
The move comes as the White House ramps up pressure on companies to hire more Americans. “Our message to Microsoft is: You’re a great American company, but you’ve got to hire great American workers,” US Vice President JD Vance said at a news conference on Thursday.
Vance claimed that the Redmond, Washington-based tech giant laid off 6,000 American workers while obtaining 6,300 H-1B foreign work visas and almost 3,000 permanent residencies known as green cards.
“You cannot lay off American workers and replace them with foreign indentured servants,” Vance said.
Across the technology sector, there have been more than 131,000 layoffs in 2026 alone, according to Layoffs.fyi, which tracks job cuts across the tech and startup industries.
“When someone comes in, they are completely subordinate to that company. If they lose that job, they have to leave the country.”
Microsoft pushed back on Vance’s claims in a blog post, saying that “80 percent of the visas were to extend or change the status of existing Microsoft employees.”
“The remaining filings for new employees were for individuals already legally in the United States who decided to come work for us, and they equal only 1% of our U.S. workforce. They are not new arrivals to our country,” the company said in the post.
Microsoft is No 5 among US employers when it comes to using H-1B visas, according to data from US Citizenship and Immigration Services. Amazon is No 1.
Adobe did not respond to Al Jazeera’s request for comment.
Secretary of Labor Keith Sonderling also said that several major information technology firms will be suspended from the programme as well. Among them are Indian tech giants Infosys and Tata Consultancy Services.
The H-1B programme has long been a target of supporters of US President Donald Trump who say that the programme undercuts American workers and is rife with abuse.
H-1B visas are meant for high-skilled jobs that are difficult to find US workers to fill. Technology companies are the biggest users, with nearly three-quarters of approvals going to workers from India.
The Department of Labor also said it had uncovered $22bn worth of visa fraud.
“Today we are protecting something far more valuable than any dollar amount. We are protecting the American worker,” Sonderling said.
The news came hours before Trump was to honour Microsoft CEO Satya Nadella with the National Medal of Technology and Innovation. India-born Nadella was one of six business leaders to be awarded on Thursday. Five of the leaders were foreign-born, including Tesla boss Elon Musk and Google co-founder Sergey Brin.
During the news conference, officials also announced a crackdown on alleged J-1 visa fraud involving nine universities, including MIT, Harvard, Yale, Stanford and Cal State, amid allegations that they brought in international students to undercut US wages. A J-1 visa allows foreign nationals to enter the US temporarily for education or cultural programmes. Universities use these visas to bring in international PhD candidates or scholars.
Vance claimed that when schools employ an American as a researcher, that person earns $20,000 more than someone brought in under a J-1 visa.
Al Jazeera asked for data supporting this claim, but the White House did not respond.
Microsoft’s stock slumped 1.5 percent on the heels of the news, while Adobe was up 2.1 percent in late day trading on Thursday.

Gambling


Tosin Akintola
|
10.8.2026 5:45 PM

On Thursday, the NFL filed an amicus brief supporting New Jersey's cert petition against Kalshi, asking the Supreme Court to clarify whether the Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC) has exclusive jurisdiction to regulate sports wagering on prediction markets.
The NFL's brief states the league is worried about ""increasing consumer harm and risk to game integrity"" caused by sports wagering on prediction markets. It accuses the CFTC and platforms like Kalshi of taking a ""laissez-faire approach"" to adopting safeguards and rules. 
In its brief, the NFL points to platforms such as Kalshi for being unwilling to adopt the league's list of prohibited wagers to guard against market manipulation and insider trading and requiring anyone wagering on sports to be at least 21 years old. The NFL also argues that the CFTC doesn't have ""adequate staff engaged in oversight and enforcement"" because the agency has only 543 employees, compared with the ""hundreds of employees covering each state's gambling industry.""
""I think the NFL is saying that they want prediction markets in the same regulatory boat as traditional sports betting to, I would guess, be able to partner with them as the NHL and Major League Baseball have already done,"" says Andrew Brandt, executive director at Villanova University's Moorad Center for Sports Law.
Unlike its counterparts in MLB, the NHL, and MLS, the NFL has so far resisted partnering with prediction markets. While MLB and the NHL have signed memorandums of understanding with the CFTC to jointly monitor sports-wagering activity on prediction markets and protect the integrity of their sports, the NFL has not. Instead, the league has inked official partnerships with sports betting companies DraftKings, FanDuel, and Fanatics Betting & Gaming. However, NFL executive vice president Jeff Miller told ESPN in March that the agency ""values the league's insights."" Still, given its partnerships with sports gambling firms, the NFL ostensibly has a vested financial interest in the issue; if the states win, a more stringent regulatory regime for prediction markets means less competition for the league's sportsbook partners.
In September, before the start of the new season, ABC News reported that the NFL sent a letter to sports prediction markets listing prohibited wagers it wanted platforms to remove. In the letter, the NFL asked prediction markets to stop ""offering objectionable bets that threaten the integrity of our games,"" such as the outcome of a field goal, a quarterback's first pass, or the yards a running back gets on his first carry.  
It's hard to see what's so objectionable about the wagers, given that the league's partners, DraftKings and FanDuel, both offer similar bets on the outcome of future plays or on who will catch a pass on a particular drive. The league's desire to limit wagers on officiating decisions—such as how many flags will be thrown in a game—out of fear of compromising a contest's integrity is understandable. Of course, the outcome of a contract matters most to the person wagering. But if an official is compromised by wagering on decisions they make as a league employee, the onus for discipline and oversight should fall on the NFL, not trading platforms.
Sports wagering is incredibly popular in America, particularly prediction markets. The 2026 American Sports Fanship Survey by the Siena Research Institute and St. Bonaventure University's Jandoli School of Communication found that 15 percent of Americans have used prediction markets to wager on sporting events. Despite their popularity with a significant segment of the population, these platforms have drawn intense backlash from state and federal lawmakers. 
This may be because prediction markets operate under federal regulations, exempting them from the fees and state taxes levied on traditional sportsbooks. ""It's grossly unfair to [traditional sportsbooks] to have [prediction markets] offer a product that's almost identical to the products that are offered by prediction markets without subjecting prediction markets to the same taxes and regulations,"" Victor Matheson, an economics and accounting professor at the College of the Holy Cross, tells Reason. 
Under federal law, the CFTC has ""exclusive jurisdiction over transactions involving swaps…traded or executed"" on prediction markets. Yet in decisions by the 6th and 9th Circuit Courts of Appeals against Kalshi, judges have declined to read the federal definition of a swap so broadly as to include the kinds of sports-event contracts offered by prediction platforms. In an amicus brief filed by 39 states and Washington, D.C., the states contend that the legal uncertainty has left them ""at an impasse over who can regulate"" prediction markets. 
In its August ruling, the 9th Circuit found Kalshi's argument that its event contracts differed from the kind of betting offered on traditional sportsbooks ""unpersuasive."" Similarly, when it ruled against Kalshi in September, the 6th Circuit found that Kalshi's sports-event contracts do not ""satisfy the statutory definition of a 'swap' to fall within the scope of the CFTC's 'exclusive jurisdiction.'"" Even ""assuming Kalshi's sports-event contracts are swaps for purposes of this analysis,"" the court found that state gambling laws are ""neither expressly nor impliedly preempted"" by federal law. 
The loss of revenue and ability to skirt state governance have clearly rankled state officials, leaving prediction markets engaged in ongoing legal battles across 20 states. At the federal level, the Government Accountability Office (GAO) is also investigating the agency, spurred by a July letter from Sen. Elizabeth Warren (D–Mass.) alleging the CFTC is ill-equipped to regulate derivative markets ""due to staffing cuts that threaten to weaken its enforcement."" 
Warren isn't the only lawmaker with the agency in their crosshairs; in its response, the GAO notes it received a ""related request"" to investigate the CFTC's reduced staffing. With the matter pitting state regulators against a federal agency, federal legislators may ultimately have to resolve the jurisdiction issue.
The CFTC is ""the most efficient regulator of prediction markets,"" writes Jacob James Rich, policy analyst at Reason Foundation, the nonprofit that publishes Reason. However, the ""threat of future presidential administrations having broad authority to change CFTC policy,"" along with legal challenges from the states, means it may not be the best long-term option for doing so. 
Kalshi has until November 9 to file a response. ESPN reported that the Supreme Court is unlikely to decide whether to take the case ""until December at the earliest."" Regardless of what the Court does, one thing is clear: Lawmakers have little trust that the adults in their states know best how to spend their money. 
Start your day with Reason. Get a daily brief of the most important stories and trends every weekday morning when you subscribe to Reason Roundup.

Tosin Akintola is an assistant editor at Reason.

86,2026-10-08,"
					By Tom Tapp 
Deputy Managing Editor

	Each year October brings Halloween, the turning of the leaves, the World Series and changing ticket prices at Disney‘s U.S. parks. 

	For 2026-2027, however, many ticket tiers remain unchanged, while those that are rising are seeing some of the smallest bumps in recent memory. There are good reasons for this.

	When Comcast reported earnings in July, it noted softer attendance at its domestic parks, despite the opening of Epic Universe in Orlando.

	At Disney, global guests grew 4% and attendance at domestic parks rose 3%. Disney noted continued, but moderating, headwinds from international attendance at domestic parks. The segment’s average per capita ticket revenue still rose 5%. Forward bookings remained robust. WDW specifically saw healthy core attendance increases from domestic tourists and annual passholders. 

	










			Watch on Deadline		







	
	
		
					Related Stories		
	













	News
	



	
		
					'Abbott Elementary' To Film 100th Episode At Disneyland		
			















	Late Night TV
	



	
		
					Guillermo Rodriguez Is Finally Getting His Moment		
			







	Still, the recently announced price points indicate Disney is aware that consumers are hurting, with gas prices making it a struggle to even get to the parks.

Disneyland‘s least expensive single-day (Tier 0) ticket is still $104. That rate has remained unchanged since 2019. In line with last year, there are eight dates to get tickets at that price — all in November. See calendars below.

	Another indication Disney is aware of a price pinch: Lowest-cost dates for each month are, this year, highlighted in green.

	The peak 1-day (Tier 6) ticket for holiday dates in Nov.-Dec. 2026 remains $224. That price currently applies to Thanksgiving week, the days around Christmas and those leading up to New Year’s Eve. See below.

	The second-highest price point, Tier 5, also remains unchanged at $199. Ditto multiday tickets, parking and annual passes. Each of the intervening tiers has only been raised $5.

	The tiers and attendant cost increases (if any) break down as follows:

Tier 0 – Was $104, still $104

Tier 1 – Was $129, now $134

Tier 2 – Was $149, now $154

Tier 3 – Was $169, now $174

Tier 4 – Was $184, now $189

	










Tier 5 – Was $199, still $199

Tier 6 – Was $224, still $224

	“Our goal is to put guests at the center of our decision making, and that starts by listening to them,” Thomas Mazloum, chairman of Disney Experiences, said of this year’s rates. “That matters more now when families are watching every dollar, and we see it in the choices they make every day — in how they plan, in how far ahead.”

	Mazloum added, “We think about pricing market by market, rather than with one broad brush,” as Disneyland guests tend to be more local and those to Walt Disney World regularly travel longer distances.

	Apropos of that, the company is still considering targeted offers in market, like last summer’s “Evening Ticket” at Disneyland for local residents. Those were only $59.

	Over in Orlando, ticket prices are generally higher, reflecting the bigger trip Walt Disney World Resort often is for guests coming from farther away. The lowest priced offering to the Magic Kingdom remains unchanged at $119 for a single day, single park ticket, though there are none on the calendar before the end of the year. The highest-priced, Tier 6, entry is also unchanged at $209. Multiday ticket prices are not increasing. 

	Annual Passes at Walt Disney World are increasing by $10-$120, depending on the level. Parking prices and Lightning Lane Multi Pass pricing are not changing.

87,2026-10-08,The head of market strategy at a London investment bank has a stark warning for investors: the artificial-intelligence trade may soon be over in what could t...
88,2026-10-08,"If not listed, please contact your TV provider.
The NFL International Series rolls on in Week 5 with the second of three games in London, and it marks the first of two consecutive contests in England for the Jacksonville Jaguars. Liam Coen's team returns to its ""home away from home"" across the Atlantic Ocean for a matchup against the Philadelphia Eagles: A contest between two teams trending in opposite directions.
If the Eagles are to save their season and return to Super Bowl contention after back-to-back losses, this is an ideal spot to prove they can beat one of the best teams in the league. If the Jaguars handle their business as favorites, they will extend their winning streak to three games and improve to above .500 all-time in games held in London.
That is not the only impactful showdown on the road to the Super Bowl this week. In fact, even more seismic collisions occur on the West Coast with an NFC West battle between the San Francisco 49ers and Seattle Seahawks and a meeting between the two Super Bowl favorites in the Los Angeles Rams and Buffalo Bills.
Here is a look inside the Super Bowl race entering Week 5.
Odds via DraftKings, +2000 or better
The next time the Jaguars play on American soil will be in November. Each of their next two games come in London as they become the first NFL team to play consecutive home regular-season games in an international market. For most teams, the departure from the friendly confines of their domestic home stadium to spend two weeks abroad could be seen as a hindrance. For the Jaguars, games in England are nothing new. They should have a logistical advantage over the Eagles and Texans, and they are simply the better team in both matchups, too.
Trevor Lawrence last week finally got a Joe Burrow-sized monkey off his back, as the Jaguars defeated the Bengals 22-17 for Lawrence's first head-to-head win over Burrow in four tries. Jacksonville now has two consecutive victories under its belt in response to its Week 2 loss and might be in the early stages of another extended winning streak. It will be favored in each of its next three games.
Lawrence is easily a top-10 quarterback, but most impressive during the 3-1 start has been the defense. The Jaguars rank second in scoring defense at just 13.3 points allowed per game. The unit stands fifth in EPA per pass despite having to match up against Burrow's high-octane aerial assault and the reigning MVP runner-up, Drake Maye, in consecutive weeks. This squad is more well-rounded than ever and looks poised to avenge last year's 35-7 loss in London with a pair of overseas wins.
The odds of Nick Sirianni bringing a second Super Bowl to the City of Brotherly Love in three years are nearly twice as long now as they were heading into Week 4. That is the product of a miserable offensive showing in which the Eagles set a franchise record with 12 third downs without a conversion en route to a 24-20 loss to the Rams. They squandered a 10-point lead in the final four minutes and have now lost two in a row after opening at 2-0. And those two wins to begin the season were hairy, one-score outcomes against bad teams.
This season is heading in the wrong direction for Sirianni. Not all of that is his fault, but he now has work to do in order to save his job, because the pressure is officially on in Philadelphia.
The Eagles closed Week 4 without 10 key players. Injuries to Saquon Barkley, DeVonta Smith, Zack Baun and numerous other starters ravaged their roster. Further depleting the squad, Lane Johnson announced his retirement from the NFL, and Philadelphia traded Cam Jurgens in an overhaul of what used to be a stout offensive line. That trade might signal that the Eagles are already prepared to pack things up and move on to next year.
With the 12-longest odds to win the Super Bowl, the market might not have caught up to the Falcons quite yet. This is no championship frontrunner, but it is a completely different team with Michael Penix Jr. running the offense, and it is one that at least deserves the respect of a division favorite.
When Cooper Rush was under center, the Falcons lost their first two games, scored a combined 16 points and coughed up seven turnovers. In the two games with Penix at quarterback, the offense racked up 80 points, managed nearly twice as many yards and turned the ball over just once. Atlanta went from 0-2 to 2-2 at the flip of a switch. Now that there is a signal-caller in the lineup capable of feeding the ball to Drake London, Bijan Robinson and Kyle Pitts, a unit that stands among the NFL's most talented collection of skill players can excel.
It is not just the offense that is getting the job done now, too. The Falcons also rank third in the league with a 43.6% pressure rate and boast the NFL's stingiest run defense by a hefty margin, having allowed just 48.3 yards per game. All of that is without Jalon Walker and James Pearce Jr. on a defensive front that was supposed to sputter in their absences.
Monday, 8:15 p.m., ABC, stream on Fubo
With respect to the Australia game between the Rams and 49ers, Week 5 presents the biggest showdown of the year thus far. Los Angeles hosts the Bills in what may be a Super Bowl preview. These two teams hold the best odds to hoist the Vince Lombardi Trophy. Both star-studded squads need a momentum-building win in this spot because the Rams did not appear nearly as strong as expected in their 2-2 start and the Bills seek to avoid a two-game losing streak on the heels of their first loss of the year.
Sunday, 4:25 p.m., Fox, stream on Fubo
The 49ers are already the top team in the NFC West with a 4-0 record and two wins in divisional play, but they can take complete command of football's best division with a win in Week 5. On the flip side, this is where the Jaguars need to pounce. Squaring off against a downtrodden team, they have a chance to build early separation in a top-heavy AFC South and cement themselves as a top-tier conference championship contender.

90,2026-10-08,"

      Singapore
  



      Singapore
  

Buyers of higher-end cars could pay up to S$30,000 more than mass-market buyers.

       This audio is generated by an AI tool.
    
SINGAPORE: The Land Transport Authority (LTA) has proposed merging categories A and B Certificates of Entitlement (COEs) for cars into a single category, and to introduce surcharges and rebates pegged to a car’s open market value.
Under the proposed fee-and-rebate system, the maximum price difference between a higher-end car and a mass-market car would be S$30,000 (US$23,000).
This means that buyers of higher-end electric vehicles (EVs) who currently pay Category A premiums, which have traditionally been lower than in Category B, could pay more as a result.
A public consultation for the proposal opened at 5pm on Thursday (Oct 8) and will end at 11.59pm on Nov 2. LTA will share its findings and recommendations by the first half of 2027.

Spot as many words as you can
